U28399 bzip2/libbzip2: internal error number %d.
U28400 This is a bug in bzip2/libbzip2, %s.
U28401 Please report it to: bzip2-devel@sourceware.org.  If this happened
U28402 when you were using some program which uses libbzip2 as a
U28403 component, you should also report this bug to the author(s)
U28404 of that program.  Please make an effort to report this bug;
U28405 timely and accurate bug reports eventually lead to higher
U28406 quality software.  Thanks.
U28407 *** A special note about internal error number 1007 ***
U28408 Experience suggests that a common cause of i.e. 1007
U28409 is unreliable memory or other hardware.  The 1007 assertion
U28410 just happens to cross-check the results of huge numbers of
U28411 memory reads/writes, and so acts (unintendedly) as a stress
U28412 test of your memory system.
U28413 I suggest the following: try compressing the file again,
U28414 possibly monitoring progress in detail with the -vv flag.
U28415 * If the error cannot be reproduced, and/or happens at different
U28416   points in compression, you may have a flaky memory system.
U28417   Try a memory-test program.  I have used Memtest86
U28418   (www.memtest86.com).  At the time of writing it is free (GPLd).
U28419   Memtest86 tests memory much more thorougly than your BIOSs
U28420   power-on test, and may find failures that the BIOS doesn't.
U28421 * If the error can be repeatably reproduced, this is a bug in
U28422   bzip2, and I would very much like to hear about it.  Please
U28423   let me know, and, ideally, save a copy of the file causing the
U28424   problem -- without which I will be unable to investigate it.
